## Safety matrix, writer W1: rules R1, R2, R3, R13

Brought to verdicts 1 to 82 by agent 07 (test updater W1) of run 4, step 3. Rules from `V4/rules.md` (R1
lines 106-116, R2 118-159, R3 161-227, R13 779-795; section 0 lines 13-102; section 2 lines 914-968;
section 3 lines 970-1073; section 4 lines 1075-1166), words from `V4/vocabulary.md` (rows `copy of` 376,
`shares` 378, `exists as` 384, `owns` 385, `using` 386, `thread_shared` 398, `weakref` 400, `ref` 123,
`ref mut` 124, `const` 95, `mut` 114, `Copy` 283, `Clone` 284, `drop` 287, `mem.drop` 258, `mem.forget`
308, `Box` 242, `Shared` 244, `Weak` 245, `SyncShared` 303, `ForeignBox` 304), the owner's verdicts in
`V4/inputs/JOB.md`, the checker's results (`V4/safety/check-V1.md`), the completeness gaps
(`V4/safety/completeness.md`) and the answers named in each row. Nothing was compiled or run. The tests
are in `V4/conformance/R1/`, `R2/`, `R3/` and `R13/` (161 files: 133 from the safety run, 28 added in
this step). Every test's `## ` comment quotes its guarantee and names its source; every refused line
carries `#$ error[CODE]: ...` and the file's `#$ help:` gives the line to write, which compiles in that
program (R18, verdict 73).

How a result is verified once the model is built, for every row:
- `compile-fail`: the conformance runner (`tests/conformance`, today's harness in
  `compiler/ember_driver/tests/`) checks the code and the message on the marked line, and the `#$ help:`
  text; one mistake gives one message (R18), so a second diagnostic fails the test.
- `compile-pass` / `run-pass`: the runner checks that it compiles with no error and that stdout equals
  the `#$ stdout:` lines.
- `run_` tests (the run-time rows of section 2): the same stdout check, and the same program built with
  AddressSanitizer and LeakSanitizer (today's sanitizer profile of `ember_rt`), which must report
  nothing: each block allocated and freed once, no read of freed memory.

"Enforced by" names the part that would enforce the rule and today's source file doing that kind of
work: parser (`compiler/ember_parser/src/body.rs`, `decls.rs`), type checker
(`compiler/ember_typeck/src/lib.rs`, `errors.rs`, `habits.rs`), borrow check (the MIR pass:
`compiler/ember_mir/src/lower.rs`, `compiler/ember_analysis/src/borrows.rs` for views and E3060/E3021,
`drops.rs` for moves and drop flags, `regions.rs` and `stores.rs` for lifetimes and E3063), effect check
(today's effect sets, `[EFF-4]`, for `changes`), code generator (`compiler/ember_codegen_c/src/lib.rs`,
`compiler/ember_codegen_llvm/src/arenas.rs`, `closures.rs`), runtime (`runtime/ember_rt/src/ember_rt.c`).

What changed in this step, by row: R1-2, R1-3, R1-6, R1-7, R1-9, R1-10 and R1-12 to R1-14; R2-2, R2-6,
R2-9, R2-11, R2-12, R2-14 to R2-17; R3-1 to R3-3, R3-8, R3-10 to R3-13, R3-16 to R3-18; R13-4 to R13-6,
R13-9. Each says which verdict or checker item changed it.

## Safety matrix, group W2: R4, R5, R6, R17 (verdicts 1 to 82)

Run 4, step 3, agent 08 (test updater for W2), from the table of safety validation (b)'s writer W2 and
its check (`check-V2.md`). Rules R4 (parameters and calls), R5 (what a first line says), R6 (views a
call gives) and R17 (inferred facts at a package's edge), with the refusals of section 3 and the
run-time checks of section 2 that belong to them, brought to verdicts 1 to 82. `V4` =
`tasks/96/redesign/run4/v4/`; `rules:N` is line N of `V4/rules.md`. The tests are in
`V4/conformance/R4/`, `R5/`, `R6/`, `R17/`: 164 files (62 `accept_`, 97 `reject_`, 5 `run_`; one
`accept_` is `compile-pass` with a warning), counted with `ls`; `R17/` also holds `ember.toml` and the
package `ledger/` its tests import. Nothing was compiled or run.

What this step changed (all in the four folders): the 15 tests and the one row `check-V2.md` marked
**wrong**, fixed as it says; its 8 **unsettled** tests and 5 unsettled rows, now settled by verdicts 63,
64, 70 and the rules (section "Settled" below); 30 existing tests changed in all; 43 tests added (the
missing tests of `completeness.md` that belong to these rules, and a `reject_` and an `accept_` for each
new refusal of verdicts 53, 54, 63, 64, 69, 70 and E0118); `R17/ledger/src/lib.em` given `HISTORY`,
`record`, `record_first` and `each`.

Today's compiler, named in "enforced by" (paths under `compiler/`):
- parser: `ember_parser/src/decls.rs` (first lines, parameter modes; today's E0110), `body.rs` (calls);
- type checker: `ember_typeck/src/lib.rs` (today's E3023, E3027, E2100, E2103, E2140, E3013, E1010);
- borrow check (the MIR pass): `ember_analysis/src/borrows.rs` (today's E3020, E3021, E3022, E3060,
  E3062); moves: `ember_analysis/src/drops.rs` (E3040); fields set in `init`:
  `ember_analysis/src/definite_init.rs` (E2100);
- effects: `ember_analysis/src/effects.rs` (today's effect sets, `[EFF-1]`..`[EFF-8]`), the nearest
  existing pass for the `changes`/`reads` checks (E2105, E2113, E2122, and the body fact of E3031 are
  new for it);
- interface file: `ember_build/src/interface.rs` (today records `effects` per body, `[EFF-4]`; the "uses
  a view parameter after a change" fact of verdict 53 would be recorded beside them);
- code generator: `ember_mir/src/lower.rs` (`lower_assert_disjoint`, line 1733; the index compare would
  be inserted here), `ember_codegen_c/src/lib.rs` (today's `restrict` kernels, `restrict_views`);
- runtime: `ember_rt`'s panic (the stops of the index compare and of `assert_disjoint`, with rules
  section 2's texts).

How a result is verified once built: `compile-fail` by the runner's expected `#$ error[CODE]: message`
on the refused line and the `#$ help:` line; `compile-pass` by exit 0 and the `#$ warning[CODE]:` line;
`run-pass` by exit 0 and one `#$ stdout:` line per printed line; `run-fail` by the `#$ panics:` text,
which is rules section 2's stop text in the program's names. The rows marked "C check" also need the
generated C inspected (today's `#$ assert-c:` header), which no test writes yet because the model's C
spelling is not fixed.

| id | guarantee | enforced by | today's tests | missing edge cases | tests | expected result and how it is verified once built |

| R5-1 | Inside a method its object is `this`, not listed among the parameters; `self`, `mut self`, `owned self` and `mut this` are refused; a `static fn` has no `this` and is called on the type; `static fn` outside a type body is refused; `this` in a free function is refused (rules:304-307, 997-998, 1003-1004, 1062; verdicts 2, 6, 7). | parser (`ember_parser/src/decls.rs`), type checker (E1010 for `this` out of scope) | `FN-4/accept_receiver_modes_and_associated_functions.em`, `CLS-2/accept_a_constructor_spelled_with_plain_self.em` (both use `self`: to be changed) | — | `reject_self_names_a_methods_object.em`, `reject_mut_self_is_changes_this.em`, `reject_owned_self_is_owns_this.em`, `reject_mut_this_on_a_methods_first_line.em`, `reject_this_in_a_static_fn.em`, `reject_this_in_a_free_function.em`, `reject_static_fn_outside_a_type.em`, `accept_a_static_fn_is_called_on_its_type.em` | compile-fail E1010 (rules:997; rules:998's form for the `static fn`, its way out now `return Counter(n=k)` because "remove `static`" leaves `Counter.starting_at(5)` refused; completeness G7's for a free function, with the call `bump(c)`; G8's two removed forms), E0113 (rules:1004's third), E2115 (rules:1003); run-pass stdout `5` |

### Tests by file (164)

`R4/` (72): every file named in the R4 rows. `R5/` (64), `R6/` (15), `R17/` (13 tests, with
`R17/ember.toml` and the package `R17/ledger/` they import). Each file's `## ` comment names its rule,
its verdict or answer, and its code.

<a id="w3"></a>

## Safety matrix, group W3: R7 (kept views) and R8 (views and changes, checked when compiling)

Run 4, step 3, agent 09 (test updater for W3), from writer W3's table of safety validation (b), brought to
verdicts 1 to 82. `V4` = `tasks/96/redesign/run4/v4/`; "line N" without a file is a line of
`V4/rules.md`; "verdict N" is the owner's verdict N in `V4/inputs/JOB.md`; "AN-N" is the answer in
`V4/area-*.md`; "check-V3" is `V4/safety/check-V3.md`; "G-N" is gap N of `V4/safety/completeness.md`.
Nothing was compiled or run (brief, "Machine limits"). Tests are in `V4/conformance/R7/` (43 files) and
`V4/conformance/R8/` (100 files): 67 `accept_`, 73 `reject_`, 3 `run_`. Every `reject_` file marks the
refused line with `#$ error[CODE]: message` and gives a line of that message in `#$ help:`; every
"line N" a message names was checked against its file (`target/redesign5/W3/lines.py`,
`target/redesign5/V3/linecheck.py`), and every `#$ help:` text was found in its message
(`target/redesign5/V3/helpcheck.py`). The files as this step found them are in
`target/redesign6/09/orig/`; the edits were made by `target/redesign6/09/r7_edits.py` and
`r8_edits.py` through `apply.py`.

**How every row is verified once the model is built.** `compile-fail`: the conformance runner checks
the code and the message on the marked line (and the `#$ help:` text). `run-pass`: the runner compares
stdout with `#$ stdout:`. `run-fail`: the runner checks the panic starts with `#$ panics:`.
`#$ assert-c-count:` lines are checked on the emitted C by today's runner
(`compiler/ember_driver/tests/milestones.rs`, `assert_c_count`). Where a row guards memory (a moved
buffer, a freed text, a freed block), its accepted programs are also run once under AddressSanitizer
(the C backend with Clang `-fsanitize=address`), which must report nothing: that shows the accepted
program is not only accepted but safe.

v3's items, and how each stands now:

1. **Open.** A generator without `from` and a change of another field of its object while it will still

Raised by this step:

13. **A method's `changes <field>` where the field holds a link.** `fn shake() changes target:` with
    `target: shares mut Player` or `sharedref mut Player` changes the player through the link. R5 (lines
    309-311) and the vocabulary's `changes` row say a field list covers "anything reached through" the
    fields; verdict 64 says a change through a link "always says `changes T`, whatever holds the link"
    (R5 lines 351-354). The tests keep `changes target` on the method (AN-48's agreed form) and write
    `changes Player` on every free function that changes the player through a link itself or through
    such a method (`main`). Does the method need `changes Player` too?
14. **The name a value was first shared from.** After `other = shares items`, `items` is one of two
    owners, a link by section 0 (lines 37-39), so `items.push(4)` is a change through a link, and `main`
    says `changes Array[int]` (`R8/accept_a_shares_owner_sees_a_change_through_another_owner.em`). Is
    that intended, or is the first owner, an own local, exempt?

    keeps AN-02's E3060.

### Section 2 checks of these rules, and their run tests

| section 2 row (line) | run test |
|---|---|
| a count +1 / -1, and before each +1 one compare with the count's maximum (926) | `C/R9/run_a_shared_object_ends_with_its_last_owner.em`; atomic: `C/R9/run_a_thread_shared_object_read_by_a_thread_and_its_maker.em`; the compare's stop: `C/R9/run_fail_too_many_owners_of_one_player_stop_the_program.em` |

Two of these checks stop the program, each with a `run_fail_` test and its stop text from section 2's
table (lines 955 and 956). The others are verified by their stdout, by reading the emitted C for the
operation the row names, and by an AddressSanitizer (ThreadSanitizer for the thread tests) run with no
report. v3's section-2 row "nothing / a deferred-drop region / a count pair" (R10) is gone: R10's region
and count pair are no longer used (verdict 82), so its two v3 run tests became the refusals of R10-2 and
R10-3. Completeness gap G15 (a `weakref` read inside R10's region) has no program left: R10 places no
region; inside R11's walk region the same read is the walk's own next step, which finds the set-aside node
ended (`C/R11/run_a_walk_whose_visit_may_end_nodes_stops_at_the_first_ended_parent.em`).

### Refusals of section 3 covered here

E3023 (a change through a `shares` owner or a `shares` field; a plain parameter behind a `shares` link),
E3020 (a shared list or a `sharedref mut` target's list changed during a loop through another owner or
holder), E3021 (a shared element under a read-only view; the walk with views), E3060 (a removal through
another owner; a name opened from a link used after a call or a loop of calls that may end its object,
by its arguments or by its type, verdicts 55 and 82), E7014, E7015 (a `shares` owner, a `sharedref mut`
holder), E7007 (two tasks through two `sharedref mut` links with `=`), E1010 (`RefCell`, `upgrade`),
E2020 (a link used unopened; an object stored without `weakref`), E2116 (`copy of` a link), E2119 (a
`weakref` of an element stored inline), E3013 (a strong parent field), E2200 (a struct viewing its
parent), and the warning L3001. Each has a `reject_` test and an `accept_` (or `run_`) test of the nearest
allowed program. Left to other writers: E0114 and E3063 for `sharedref mut` (R7, W3); E3023 for
`shares mut x` made from a read-only owner (R3, W1); E2105 for a change through a link not named (R5, W2)
and for a function that ends class objects of its own state (R12, W5); E7004, E7017 and E7018 (R15, W6).

  `reject_copy_of_a_link.em` (`@derive(Clone)` on `Player`); `reject_upgrade_is_not_ember.em`
  (`println(p.hp)`, and the message names the lines it replaces).
- check-V4's two unsettled tests and rows R10-2, R10-3 settled by verdict 82: v3's
  `run_a_view_kept_across_a_loop_of_calls_that_may_end_players.em` and
  `run_a_view_opened_from_a_link_keeps_its_player_across_a_call_that_ends_it.em` became

- check-V4's open items settled: R10-1's function value whose own state owns a `Player` (verdict 55:
  R10-4's second pair); a `copy of` a struct holding a `weakref` (verdict 79: R11-5's run test); the
  message for a type without `Clone` (rules section 3's E2116 row).
- completeness.md's gaps of these rules: G14 (verdict 56: R11-12), G15 (no program left, above), G16
  (verdicts 58, 59: R9-13, R11-13), G17's `weakref` half (verdict 79: R11-5).

- Spelling to verdicts 1 to 82 (the paragraph above the table).

## Safety matrix, writer W5: R12 (function values)

Run 4, step 3 (agent 11, test updater for W5), brought to verdicts 1 to 82. Rule: R12, `V4/rules.md`
lines 600-777, with the section 3 refusals and the section 2 run-time rows that belong to it. Verdicts
from `V4/inputs/JOB.md`. Tests: `V4/conformance/R12/`, 115 tests (61 `reject_`, 49 `accept_`, 5 `run_`)
and three modules the tests import (`widgets.em`, `logbook.em`, `log_widgets.em`). Nothing was compiled
or run (brief, "Machine limits"). `V4` = `tasks/96/redesign/run4/v4/`; "line N" with no file is a line of
`V4/rules.md`. Step 2's matrix (v3 line numbers, 95 tests) is kept at `target/redesign6/11/orig/`.

What this step changed, in short: the twelve tests `check-V5.md` marked **wrong** are fixed (a missing
`changes` on `main`, helps that were not the message's text, messages whose way out did not compile);
its three **unsettled** tests are settled (verdict 71) or no longer depend on the open point (the
`dump(log, note)` call, `for_owner`); completeness gaps G5, G8 (`owned fn`) and G10 are written; new
rows R12-27 to R12-32 cover verdicts 55, 61, 71, 75, 80 and E0120, and verdict 76 is added to R12-9 and
R12-10; verdict 73 moved the walk-under-the-lock test from E3020 to E7018; every test's citations of the
rules now give v4 line numbers.

### How to read this

- One section per guarantee, `R12-1` .. `R12-32`, with the seven fields of the brief.
- **today's tests** are `tests/conformance/<RULE>/<file>.em`. Those that expect the opposite of the new
  rule are marked **(superseded)**.
- **enforced by** names today's source file that does this kind of work now. Short names used below:
  - `typeck`: `compiler/ember_typeck/src/lib.rs` (the type checker; captures are resolved at
    `resolve_capture`, about line 25452, and checked from about line 49099).
  - `effects`: `compiler/ember_analysis/src/effects.rs` (today's effect sets, `[EFF-1]`..`[EFF-6]`; it
    says itself that a call through a function value "contributes every effect: no function type
    declares its effects yet").
  - `borrows`: `compiler/ember_analysis/src/borrows.rs` (the borrow check, the MIR pass that today
    checks borrows and regions; it emits E3020, E3021, E3022, E3060; captures from about line 2257).
  - `stores`: `compiler/ember_analysis/src/stores.rs` (emits E3026 today).
  - `drops`: `compiler/ember_analysis/src/drops.rs` (emits E3013 today).
  - `parser`: `compiler/ember_parser/src/body.rs` and `decls.rs`.
  - `codegen`: `compiler/ember_codegen_c/src/lib.rs` and `compiler/ember_codegen_llvm/src/closures.rs`
    (today's function value is `ember_fn_value`, `{native, descriptor, w[3]}`, 40 bytes).
  - `runtime`: `runtime/ember_rt/src/ember_rt.c`.
  - `interface`: `compiler/ember_build/src/interface.rs` (a package's interface file, R17).
  E2105, E2113, E0120 and E2020's lambda-parameter case are not in today's compiler (`grep` over
  `compiler/` finds none of the first three): the checks are new work, most naturally beside `effects`.
- **How a test is verified once built**: a `compile-fail` test by the runner's expected code and
  message on the marked line and its `#$ help:` line (`compiler/ember_driver/tests/milestones.rs`:
  `assert_exact_diagnostics`, a diagnostic no annotation claims fails the test; `assert_diagnostic_helps`,
  each `#$ help:` is text of the diagnostic's help); `run-pass` by `#$ stdout:`; the five `run_` tests,
  and the `accept_` tests that dispatch handlers, also by a build of the emitted C under
  AddressSanitizer with no report, as `V3/area-D.md` AN-60, AN-61 and AN-62 checked the same shapes in C.
- Every message was checked against R18 and verdict 73: the lines it gives compile in that program (the
  `accept_` test named beside each `reject_` test is, in most rows, that program after the fix). Where
  a message is the rules' own, it is section 3's text in the program's names; where the fix also needs a
  line the rules' example did not have (`main`'s first line), the test's message names it too.
- Where a named function's first line is missing a change, the E2105 marker is on its first line (the
  line the message asks to change); the rules do not say which line E2105 is reported on (`for_owner`).

---

#### R12-1. A plain `fn()` changes nothing at all; a lambda names the module variables it changes

| field | value |
|---|---|
| guarantee | A lambda whose body changes a module variable says so on its first line (`fn() changes LOG:`), so no function value hides a change of `LOG` behind a type that says it changes nothing. |
| source | R12, lines 601-603, 611, 622-626; verdict 17; section 3 line 989 (E2105) |
| enforced by | type checker (`typeck`), with the change sets of `effects`; parser for `changes` after a lambda's parameters |
| today's tests | `EFF-1/accept_a_call_through_a_plain_fn_value_has_the_effects_of_its_functions.em` (effects of a call through a function value are those of the functions of its shape: the opposite approach, inferred, not written) |
| missing edge cases | a lambda that changes a module variable only through a call it makes (`fn(): tick()`), covered by R5's "the calls it makes included" but not tested here; a lambda inside a lambda; a named local function (verdict 51) that changes `LOG` with no clause (only the `Mutex` case, R12-6, is tested) |
| tests | `reject_a_lambda_that_pushes_to_log_without_saying_so.em`, `accept_a_lambda_that_says_it_pushes_to_log.em` |
| expected result | reject: compile-fail E2105 "this function pushes to `LOG`; say so on its first line: `fn() changes LOG: LOG.push(String("clicked"))`" (line 989, word for word); accept: run-pass, stdout `1` |

| field | value |
|---|---|
| guarantee | A capture with no word is the real variable (row 6); a lambda that changes it names it (`fn() changes score:`), the change is seen by the function that made it, and a change by that function is seen by the lambda. |
| source | R12 lines 601-603, 741-748; row 6; verdicts 22, 80 ("without a word a function only borrows"); `V3/area-D.md` line 43; AN-53; section 3 line 989 |
| enforced by | type checker (`typeck`, capture resolution) for the clause; borrow check (`borrows`) for what the place that holds the lambda reaches |
| today's tests | `CLO-2/accept_mutating_a_captured_local.em` (a capture written in place); `CLO-2/reject_outer_write_while_mutating_capture_is_live.em` and `CLO-12/reject_a_capture_written_while_the_local_function_lives.em` **(superseded)**: today a write by the maker while the lambda lives is E3021, under row 6 it is seen by the lambda |
| missing edge cases | a real variable that is a list read by the lambda while the maker reshapes it between calls (the lambda holds no view across calls, so nothing to refuse, but untested); two lambdas sharing one real variable |
| tests | `reject_row_6_handler_without_changes_score.em`, `accept_row_6_handler_changes_the_real_score.em`, `run_a_real_variable_is_seen_by_the_handler_and_back.em` |
| expected result | reject: compile-fail E2105 "this function changes `score`, a variable it uses from `game`; say so on its first line: `b.on_click(fn() changes score: score += 1)`" (line 989); accept: run-pass `1`; run: run-pass `1`, `11`, ASan clean |

|---|---|

#### R12-29. A lambda's parameter says what it receives, matching the function type (verdict 71)

| field | value |
|---|---|

| expected result | compile-fail E2020 ("`each_name` hands each name over (`fn(owns String)`), so this function receives it as its own: write `fn(owns s) => println(s.len())`", word for word); run-pass `3`, `1`; compile-fail E2020 ("`each_name` gives each name read-only, so this function cannot take it over; to keep your own, write `fn(s) changes names: names.push(copy of s)`", word for word, completeness G5); run-pass `1` |

<a id="w6"></a>

## Safety matrix, group W6: R14, R15, R16 and the C side

Run 4, step 3, agent 12 (test updater for group W6), from the matrix writer W6 wrote in safety validation
(b) and its checker's results (`V4/safety/check-V6.md`). Rules: R14 (interface values, kind tests,
casts, and verdict 46's `dyn ... changes`), R15 (threads), R16 (`unsafe`), and the C side of R12 and R15
(kept callbacks, `@ffi(no_callbacks)`, the checked trampoline, variadic calls; verdicts 31, 39, 62).
Sources: `V4/rules.md` (cited as `rules.md:N`, the v4 file as agent 01 left it), `V4/vocabulary.md`,
`V4/changes.md`, `V4/inputs/JOB.md` (verdicts 1 to 82), `V4/inputs/worklist.md`, `V4/safety/check-V6.md`,
`V4/safety/completeness.md`. Nothing was compiled or run.

The tests are in `V4/conformance/R14/` (38), `V4/conformance/R15/` (116, and the C library of the
trampoline and callback tests, `kept_events.c` with `kept_events.h`) and `V4/conformance/R16/` (16, and
the C function of the variadic tests, `wide_sum.c` with `wide_sum.h`): 170 tests (60 `accept_`, 91
`reject_`, 19 `run_`). Line numbers inside expected messages and stop texts are the test file's own lines
(checked by `target/redesign6/12/check_lines.py`, which prints the text of every line a message names,
output `check_lines.txt`; the files are written by `target/redesign6/12/gen.py` from `tests_r14.py`,
`tests_r15.py`, `tests_r15_c.py`, `tests_r16.py`, which reproduce v3's files byte for byte before this
step's edits). Where a guarantee is also another group's, the row names that group's test (`R9/...`, W4).

How every result is verified once the model is built: the conformance runner (`ember test`,
`compiler/ember_driver/src/test_runner.rs`) compiles each file and compares the output with its `#$`
lines: for `compile-fail` the code and the message of each `#$ error` on its line, and `#$ help:`; for
`compile-pass` the `#$ warning` lines; for `run-pass` the stdout lines in order; for `run-fail` the
stdout before the stop and the start of the stop's text (`#$ panics:`). The thread tests are also run
once under ThreadSanitizer and the trampoline, callback and cast tests under AddressSanitizer (C backend,
Clang), which must report nothing: that shows the run-time checks stop the program before memory is
touched wrongly, and that the compile-time checks leave no data race.

"Enforcing part": parser `compiler/ember_parser/src/body.rs`, `decls.rs` (lexer
`compiler/ember_lexer/src/token.rs`); type checker `compiler/ember_typeck/src/lib.rs` (old spellings in
`compiler/ember_typeck/src/habits.rs`); borrow check = the MIR passes of `compiler/ember_analysis/src/`
(`borrows.rs`, `regions.rs`, `stores.rs` for E3026, `coroutines.rs` for E2221, `static_safe.rs` for
E4030, `effects.rs` for today's effect sets, the nearest existing work to checking `changes`); MIR
lowering `compiler/ember_mir/src/lower.rs` (`lower_class_downcast`), `compiler/ember_mir/src/parallel.rs`;
code generator `compiler/ember_codegen_c/src/lib.rs` (the downcast stop, `AssertKind::Downcast`; the C
trampolines), `compiler/ember_codegen_llvm/src/ffi.rs` and `dispatch.rs`; interface files
`compiler/ember_build/src/interface.rs` (R17); runtime `runtime/ember_rt/src/ember_rt.c`
(`ember_downcast`, locks, thread checks). The codes section 4 marks bold (not in today's
`compiler/ember_diag/src/codes.rs`) are emitted nowhere today; the row names the part that would.

Checked and left as they were, by verdict: no test of these rules changes an object through a `shares`,
`shares mut` or `thread_shared` link held by a `ref mut` parameter or an own local without `changes T`
but the parallel loop above (verdicts 63, 64); none passes a part of what a callee changes to a view
parameter it uses afterwards (verdicts 53, 54); no lambda parameter mode, `const ref mut` call, `drop`,
`mem.forget`, map or string walk, or `copy of` of a struct with links appears (verdicts 58, 68 to 72,
78, 79); every lambda that changes a captured `Atomic` names the variable (verdict 75); no function value
outlives its maker without `with owns` (verdict 80); no old spelling (`holds`, `copied`, `owned`,
`shared`, `weak`, `self`, `Box`, `.copied()`, `set by`, `Some(...)` in a header) is left (a search of the
three folders).

### Open points (each is in the form's `for_owner`)

1. **The trampoline tag's layout.** `run_c_holds_a_tagged_number_not_an_address.em` checks AN-78's
   `0x4080` in the top 16 bits; the rules say only "a tagged `{slot, generation}` number, never an
   address" (`rules.md:866-868`). No verdict settles it.
2. **The stop of a used-up `callback(once)` called again.** Section 2's table (`rules.md:958-959`) has
   texts for a call after the token ended and for a wrong thread, none for this case;
   `run_fail_c_calls_a_once_callback_twice.em` checks only the start both texts share ("C called the
   callback registered on line 29").
3. **E5013's message says what `sum_wide` reads** (`int64_t`), which the compiler cannot see in C; R18
   (`rules.md:912`) says a message says only what the compiler knows. The test uses the worklist's text
   (section 4, item 12) as given.
4. **A `thread_shared` object whose class has a field of a plain function type.** Verdict 61 says such
   fields "use such types" (`thread_shared fn()`); no code or line is given for one that does not, so no
   test is written for it.
5. **A scoped task's loop name without `with copy of`: E3026.** R15 (`rules.md:836-840`) puts the loop's
   own variable and a round local in one sentence and names no code; the test uses the round local's
   (AN-73's E3026). For an unscoped thread E7004 applies (no capture word).
6. **Verdict 65's copy for a string loop variable.** `with copy of t` for `for t in texts:` copies each
   `String` into its task (one allocation per task), where Rust's scoped threads borrow `&t`; the tests
   follow the verdict as written.
7. **A plain `thread_shared Log` argument beside a `fn() changes Log` argument of one call** (check-V6
   item 10, `V4/changes.md` section 12 item 5): `reject_a_call_through_a_function_value_that_may_take_the_held_lock.em`,
   `reject_a_function_value_that_changes_another_log_inside_the_lock.em`,
   `accept_the_function_value_called_after_the_lock_is_let_go.em` and
   `reject_a_walk_under_the_lock_across_a_function_value_that_changes_log.em` take R4 (`rules.md:268-277`)
   to allow it; no verdict settles it.
8. **`owns` on the making line of an `Option[owns T]` field** (verdict 74's reach): the generator tests
   write `next=Some(owns Link(value=1))`; the verdict names `owns T` fields, not an `Option` of one.
9. **A change a callee names by a type, on the caller's own local** (`V4/changes.md` section 12 item 3):
   `accept_a_task_takes_the_lock_itself.em` follows R5 as written (`main` and the task say
   `changes Mutex[Array[int]]`).
10. **Which line carries E2040 when two operations of a `pub` body need the missing bound** (check-V6's
    U6): the test now uses section 3's one-operation example (`rules.md:1001`); R14 (`rules.md:818-819`)
    still does not say it for two.
11. **E7010's second way out and E7015.** Section 3's E7010 message (`rules.md:1056`) offers guarding
    the fields of `shares mut Player` objects that `@parallel` chunks on other threads change through
    `ref mut` views; R9 (`rules.md:484-485`) says a `shares` value never reaches another thread (E7015).
    Whether a chunk's view of such an object counts as reaching it is not said; if it does, the way out
    runs into E7015 (verdict 73). The test keeps the message (its first way out compiles).
